# Tankbook – Task Backlog

*`PHASES.md` broken into agent-sized tasks. Each task names its deliverable and the **checks that make it done** – a task without its checks green is not done, and checks land in the same PR as the code (TESTING.md levels: L1 unit · L2 contract · L3 sync-scenario · L4 UI/snapshot · L5 accuracy · M manual). Task IDs are stable – reference them in branches/PRs (`p0-4-grdb-migrations`).*

## Implied in every task's checks: it builds and it lints

The per-task checks below are **in addition to** the baseline gate, which is never restated per row because it applies to all of them: every tier a task touches must compile and its linter must exit **0** (`CLAUDE.md` hard rule 14, `docs/TESTING.md` → the baseline gate). Run `swiftlint lint` **from the repo root**, and judge it by exit code. A task whose feature tests pass but which leaves the build broken or the linter red is **not** done and does not get an `[x]`.

## Status legend

`[x]` done and independently verified – its own checks **and** the baseline gate re-run outside the implementing agent · `[~]` partially landed, gap named · `[ ]` not started · `[!]` **blocked on an action only the product owner can take** (a purchase, a legal decision, an account) - not work an agent can pick up · `[cut]` **decided against, with the reason recorded in the row and in the owning doc** – a cut task is not work waiting to be picked up, and re-proposing it needs the recorded reason to have changed. Status is recorded here so a fresh session knows what to pick up without re-deriving it.

## Version scope

*`CLAUDE.md` → Version scope is the convention. **A row without a marker is v1** – the launch build owes it, and the launch triage below says in which tier. Rows marked **[v1.0.x]**, **[v1.1]**, **[v1.x]** or **[v2]** are deferred to that release; the marker repeats the triage's target so a row can be read on its own. Section AG is v2 in its entirety.*

## Index – every task, its title, and where it lives

*Generated by `scripts/tasks-index.py` from the rows themselves – **rebuild it whenever a row is**
**added or ticked**, and never edit the table by hand. `below` means this file, under the section
named; `TASKS-DONE.md` means `docs/TASKS-DONE.md`, which carries the same section headings. The
dispatch ledger moved to `docs/TASKS-HISTORY.md`.*

## Launch triage (2026-08-29) – what v1 needs, what waits

*Every open row (P-, W-, PR-, PJ-) sorted against one question: **can the app be submitted and
used by a stranger without this?** Three tiers. **Blocker** – submission is impossible or the
first session breaks a hard rule visibly. **Required** – a v1 promise (`VISION.md` MVP row, a
journey "always/never", or a written security/privacy commitment) that the code does not keep;
ship without it only with a written product-owner exception. **Deferred** – real, tracked, and
not a v1 condition; target named. Sizes are relative (S under a day of agent work, M a few days,
L a week-plus). Ordering inside a tier is the recommended sequence.*

**Two decisions this triage assumes, each reversible by the product owner:**

1. **The backend ships with v1.** `api.tankbook.live` is deliberately 502 today. Without it the
   app loses not only sync and cloud extract but **import parsing** (server-side by rule 9),
   **fresh exchange rates** (the seed pack ages from day one), remote config and the `appUpdate`
   notice – three MVP rows and the only way to retire a bad build. The backend is built and
   L2/L3-tested (P4, P5 complete); what is missing is the deployment itself, which no row tracks.
2. **v1 ships what is built, not only VISION's MVP column.** Service, reminders and sync are
   "v1.x" in `VISION.md` but P3 and P4 are complete; they ship, so their unreachable or
   half-wired parts count as required. EV charging (J6) and the Pro tier stay out.

### Tier 1 · Blockers (nothing ships without these)

| Order | Task | Why it blocks | Size |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **[x] PJ.1** capture pipeline (`520ea0c`) + **[x] PJ.2** keep the photo | The hero feature: no camera or Photos image ever becomes a `ConfirmPrefill`. A "scan, don't type" app whose scan door is painted on is not this product, and P2's own exit gate (5 live fill-ups < 15 s) cannot be measured until it exists | L |
| 2 | **[x] SH.1** deploy the backend (closed 2026-09-14, product owner) | Decision 1. Import, rates, config, gateway, sync all route through it | M |
| 3 | **[x] PR.17** rate limiting + body caps · **[x] PR.18** presign constraints + orphan sweep · **[x] PR.34** refuse to start with dev secrets (`2b83a89`) | Must land *before* SH.1 is public: unauthenticated `/import/parse` can fill blob storage at line rate today, and a presigned PUT accepts any bytes of any size | S+S+S |
| 4 | **[x] PR.3** config layer live, closes P0.12 (`0914a33` + `1f0f71c`); PR.3c deferred | With the fetcher `nil` and nine inline base-URL fallbacks, no kill switch, quota or `appUpdate` can reach a device – there is no way to retire a broken build without a store release | M |
| 5 | **[x] PR.1** token refresh · **[x] PR.2** sign-out revokes (`682f3d4`) · **[x] PJ.13** first push after sign-in (`38005cc`) | Sign-in ships (decision 2) and every account stops syncing ~60 min after sign-in with a false "update the app"; the second device restores an empty account. Alternative if time is short: hide Sign in for v1 and defer all three – say so in `VISION.md` | M+S+S |
| 6 | **[x] PJ.10** import date-format question · **[x] PJ.9** non-fill rows commit · **[x] PJ.11** validation on every write | Import is MVP; today an ambiguous D/M file silently imports every row as M/D – the exact stats-poisoning misread J2 warns about – and service/import rows bypass the timeline check | S+S+S |
| 7 | **[x] PR.6** transport timeouts | 60 s frozen Sign in / Sync now / Import on a half-connected radio, with no cancel | S |
| 8 | **[x] PR.5** app logging through the redactor | Forty `.public` error-description log sites outside the redactor; the privacy promise (hard rule 12) is upheld by a GRDB default, not by our code | S |
| 9 | **[x] P6.6** store assets EN/RU, privacy labels, TestFlight ring (closed 2026-09-14, product owner); the ERRORS.md coverage walk it carries | Submission itself. Two declarations still need the owner (`ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption`, and `UIBackgroundModes` only with PJ-level push wiring – which is deferred, so it stays absent) | M |
| 10 | **[~] SH.2** release build path (new row below) | iOS CI is disabled and never completed a run; a TestFlight build needs a reproducible archive from a clean checkout | S |

### Tier 2 · Required for v1 (promised, cheap relative to the promise)

| Order | Task | Promise it keeps | Size |
|---|---|---|---|
| 11 | **[x] PJ.3** Welcome root with three paths | `SCREENMAP.md` root; J1/J11: a reinstall or migrant is funnelled into "Add your first car"; the wrong-provider detection can never fire | M |
| 12 | **[x] PJ.14** live "+N km since last" | Named in the `VISION.md` MVP fill-up row and `DESIGN.md:67` | S |
| 13 | **[x] PJ.17** empty-but-alive Confirm (with PJ.1) | F1: the failure state *is* the manual form, keyboard on Total, photo kept | S |
| 14 | **[x] PJ.8** rate backfill trigger | F9 / multi-currency MVP: pending rates stay pending forever today | S |
| 15 | **[x] PJ.4** + **[x] PJ.5** Reminders reachable, notification tap routed | P3 is "COMPLETE" and the screen has no production entry point; a fired notification lands nowhere | S+S |
| 16 | **[x] PJ.6** Type-it is mode-aware · **[x] PJ.12** hide the dead Charge chip · **[x] P2.3b** fuel row offers only the car's fuels | Hard rules 15 and 13 on the Confirm/Capture surfaces | S+S+S |
| 17 | **[x] PJ.36** "Export everything" works (or the row goes) · **[x] PJ.38** CSV export | `VISION.md:42` "one-tap CSV/JSON export – always free"; a dead row is a dead end | S+S |
| 18 | **[x] PR.14** real "Changed by sync" + batch toast · **[x] PR.13** offline vs server-down · **[x] PR.4** persist `SyncPayloadMemory` | Hard rule 8 and hard rule 13 once sync ships; overwrites are stored but invisible, and a stale device can revert an edit | M+S+S |
| 19 | **[x] PR.8** trace + client version on the wire | The only way a support report maps to a server line; without it every field bug is a guess | S |
| 20 | **[x] PR.7** retry with backoff | The 429 notice promises "Retrying in N minutes" and nothing retries | S |
| 21 | **[x] PJ.20** About & feedback (feedback row + improve-scanning consent) | `ERRORS.md` About; P6.10 and the import wizard both route "send us this case" to a screen that does not exist | S |
| 22 | **[x] PJ.33** per-source export guide (MFM at minimum) | With only one importer shipping (see deferred P5.4b), the guide is what tells a switcher what works | S |
| 23 | **[x] P6.13** RU clips at Dynamic Type XL · **[x] P6.5** accessibility audit | `DESIGN.md` accessibility floor; P6's exit gate | S+M |
| 24 | **[x] PJ.7** reminders in Recently deleted + honest alert copy · **[x] P1.13** grouped odometer · **[x] PJ.47** doc reconciliation | Hard rule 8 copy that currently lies; a formatter bypass; the ledger's own honesty | S+S+S |
| 25 | **[x] PR.16** explicit file protection + the promised test | `SECURITY.md` names it as enforcement; met by platform default today, so last in this tier | S |

### The v1.1 priority queue (product owner, 2026-08-31)

Eleven rows pulled out of the v1.1 bucket and **ordered**. Version markers are unchanged - these
stay `[v1.1]`/`[v1.x]` - this is a sequencing decision, not a scope change. Rows are marked
**PRIORITY** in place.

**Why these eleven.** Seven are the J7 cluster: P3 built the nouns (service entries, parts, tire
sets, reminders) and skipped most of the verbs that connect them, which is how the phase reads
COMPLETE while the journey does not hold together. Four are already-built machinery that nothing
calls - the cheapest rows in the backlog.

| # | Task | What the user hits today |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **[ ] PJ.28** | **Expense capture photographs the receipt and discards it.** Half delivered by [RV.62] (2026-09-04): the shutter is no longer decorative and total/currency/date DO pre-fill. What remains is the PHOTO - `ExpenseEntryView.swift:189` still saves `attachments: []`, so the image is read and then thrown away. Still the only row here that silently loses data |
| 2 | **[ ] PJ.22** | **The service log never proposes the next service.** No lifetime editor, so `proposedReminderId` is always nil. J7's promise is "the app proposes the next reminder"; today you log the past and remember the future yourself |
| 3 | **[ ] PJ.24** | **One door at completion.** The ReminderComplete sheet offers Type and Skip while the invoice is in the user's hand - hard rule 15 says the camera is a peer at every entry point |
| 4 | **[ ] PJ.23** | **A service line is frozen once saved.** Title, category and cost are uneditable, so a typo means delete and re-enter, losing attachments and links. `.other` "promoted later without data loss" has no promotion path |
| 5 | **[ ] PJ.26** | **Tires and their price never meet.** `purchaseExpenseId` exists in the schema and migration and is written **nowhere** - a tire set cannot say what it cost |
| 6 | **[ ] PJ.27** | **Nothing reminds you to swap back in spring.** A mount is recorded, then silence for six months |
| 7 | **[ ] PJ.25** | **The parts shelf is unreachable from the Garage** - it works, but only opens from inside a service entry |
| 8 | **[ ] PJ.19** | Station suggestion: the logic is **written** in `JOURNEYS.md`/`SCHEMA.md` and never called |
| 9 | **[ ] PJ.34** | F9a already ranks suggestions; the UI does not consume them, so receipt/QR date priority is computed and discarded |
| 10 | **[ ] PJ.35** | **[v1.1]** `.blobPrefetch` is a `PowerWorkKind` case **nothing produces** (P6.20) - photos never prefetch |
| 11 | **[ ] PJ.45** | `paceLimitKmPerDay` is a real per-vehicle field **edited nowhere in the app** |

Rows 8-11 are the pattern worth watching: **the mechanism exists, tested, and no user can reach
it.** Ask what produces a case, not only what handles it - the same question that produced P6.20.

| **[~]** W8 | **FIXED 2026-08-28 alongside P6.7: light `headlight` moved to a value clearing 4.5:1 on both light grounds (5.62:1 and 6.08:1), pinned by a test that fails at the old value with the exact 4.217:1. Screenshots outstanding with P6.7.** **The light-theme tokens fail WCAG AA, and this is an APP bug before it is a site bug.** Computed from `design/tokens.json`: light `headlight` on light `midnight` is **4.22:1** and light `midnight` on light `taillight` is **4.32:1**, both under the 4.5:1 floor `DESIGN.md` sets. The site's button was fixed token-legally (foreground moved to `--dash`, 4.67:1), but the **link colour cannot be fixed without changing the token**, and that token is shared with the iOS app - so the same pair is likely failing in the app's light theme, where no test looks. Changing it means re-verifying the app and reconciling `SiteLanding.dc.html`, which draws the failing pair | Contrast computed, not eyeballed, for every token pair in **both** themes, reported as numbers; the app's light theme re-checked at the same pairs; the artboard updated in the same change |
| W9 | **Russian copy defects on the site**, found by reading the rendered pages. `roadmap.ru.md` «по желательному аккаунту» means *the account we recommend*, not *optional*; `privacy.ru.md` «устроен локально-первым» is a calque that cannot attach grammatically; `support.ru.md` «приложить сам случай» - you attach a file, not a case; `_index.ru.md` «голубым фар» is broken agreement; **электрокар** and **электромобиль** are used for the same thing on one site; and the hero caption quotes the app's button as «Ввести» when the real label is «Ввести вручную» - a caption whose entire point is "this is the real app" misquoting it. Held pending the product owner, who asked that texts stay as they are for now | A native read of both rendered pages; one term for EV site-wide; the caption matches `Localizable.xcstrings` exactly |
| W10 | **[v1.0.x]** **`SITE.md` states a rule its own artboard breaks.** The doc makes "two CTAs of equal weight" a design rule; `SiteLanding.dc.html` draws the hero CTAs primary/secondary and the built site follows the artboard. Hard rule 15 is **not** implicated - these two CTAs are mailto vs roadmap, not the two entry paths, and the doors section carries the entry-path equality correctly. But a doc rule the shipped design contradicts is exactly the drift `CLAUDE.md`'s conflict rule says to fix in the same change | `SITE.md` and the artboard agree; whichever way it resolves, the reason is written down |


**`api.tankbook.live` does not answer yet, and that is intentional** (product owner, 2026-08-28). nginx proxies it and returns
**502** because no backend is listening on the upstream port. Postponed deliberately, not broken: the app is local-first, so an
unreachable API costs sync, cloud extract and import parse and **nothing else** - every screen keeps working offline (hard rule 1).
Two consequences worth knowing while it stays down: the config guardrail's `GET /health` probe cannot promote a candidate
`apiBaseUrl`, so the bundled default stands; and P6.18's update requirement resolves `.none`, because a config document that never
arrives carries no `appUpdate` - which is the fail-open rule behaving exactly as designed.


**Two open product decisions, neither of them engineering:** the analytics choice (none / the CDN
operator's own / a hosted cookieless service - see `SITE.md`), and whether **RU 152-ФЗ** obligations
apply alongside GDPR. W2 ships GDPR-framed with the second question tracked, not guessed at.

**Three Russian copy items** found by reading the rendered artboard, held pending the product owner:
`БЕСПЛАТНО … БЕСПЛАТНЫЙ` repeats a root in one strip; `Топливо, зарядки, сервис и всё остальное
хранится` takes a singular verb on a plural list; `как удобно` reads clipped against `как вам удобно`.

## PJ · Journeys review (2026-08-29)

*Source: `docs/JOURNEYS.md` reviewed stage by stage against the tree at `93d2619` by four
read-only passes (brief `agents/briefs/REVIEW-JOURNEYS.md`); every bug-level claim below was
re-verified by hand before it was written. Gaps already tracked elsewhere are cited, not re-filed:
PR.2 (sign-out revoke), PR.7 (auto-retry), PR.13 (offline vs server-down), PR.14 (S1 row + S7
toast - its deliverable should also name Recently deleted's "Overwritten by sync" section and
Compare, both fixtures), PR.22 (LLM quota note), PR.30 (reminder hours), P2.6/P2.7/P5.5b/P6.9
open halves. J6 (EV charge) and J12 (second driver) are v1.x/v2 per `VISION.md` and are not
filed, except for the one live defect J6 leaves in v1 (PJ.12).*

*Five ticked rows claim more than the code does; PJ.47 footnotes them: **P1.1** (Welcome root
and its three edges were never built), **P2.2/P2.3** (parser ported, sheet takes a prefill - the
seam camera -> OCR -> prefill -> sheet was never a task), **P3.4** (Reminders has no production
entry point), **P4.4** (wrong-provider detection exists, the entry that arms it does not),
**P5.2** (S8 backfill runs only under a DEBUG flag), **P6.3** (only the timeout branch of F4
renders).*

*The shape of the finding, in one sentence: the Confirm sheet, the engines and the import review
are built and L4-covered, but **the scan door is painted on** - no camera or Photos image ever
becomes a `ConfirmPrefill` outside launch-argument seeds - and four screens that the journeys
route through (Welcome, Reminders, the S5 notice, the post-sign-in push) exist only behind
`-force*` fixtures.*

A standing instruction nobody measures is a wish | `scripts/scenario-index.py` (or `tasks-index.py --check`) reads the run history table in `agents/briefs/REVIEW-JOURNEYS.md` (the `Tree` column carries the commit) and counts `[x]` rows ticked since it; at 10 or more it prints a WARNING naming the count and the brief; `--check` still exits 0 - the walk is a decision, not a build break | L1 (the script's own tests, `scripts/tests/`): a fixture history with a tree 12 rows back warns, 9 rows back is silent; **mutation**: break the count and the warning test goes red |

| **[ ]** PJ.49 | **[v2]** **An EV can be garaged but cannot log a single charge, while both listings sell "electric in one history".** Found 2026-09-06 in the feature-gap review (`diagnostics/RESEARCH-feature-gaps-qwen.md`, G1), verified in code: `CaptureMode.modes(for: .ev)` returns `[.service, .expense]` (`CaptureMode.swift:44-47`), and outside test seeds the ONLY place that constructs a `ChargeSession` is the EDIT screen (`EditEntryView.swift`) - there is no creation path anywhere. `ConsumptionEngine` reads charge sessions for the EV figure, so an EV's headline number can never fill, and `HomeSections.swift` already renders kWh for sessions nobody can create. **The schema is not the problem**: `ChargeSession`, `Tariff`, the engine, persistence, sync and log rendering all exist and are tested (`docs/SCHEMA.md`). Say what a partial charge means and whether a session without kWh is allowed, since the engine has to divide by it. **Deferring this is a decision about the LISTING, not only the backlog** - see PJ.51 | L1: an EV with two charge sessions produces a kWh/100 figure equal to the engine's own for the same inputs. L1: a `.phev` keeps both doors. L4 `CaptureUITests`: an EV car's capture surface offers Charge as its primary mode; saving one lands it in the log with its kWh. **Vacuous traps**: asserting the form renders without asserting the session PERSISTS; testing with a `.phev`, where the fill-up door hides the gap; asserting the mode list rather than what saving produces. Screenshots EN+RU |
| **[ ]** PJ.52 | **[v2]** **The parts shelf J6 already describes, and the schema already carries.** Raised by the product owner 2026-09-06 as promising, and the groundwork is done: `ServiceRecord.usedParts: [UUID]` links `Expense(.parts)` entries installed in a service ("links, not costs", `docs/SCHEMA.md`), `ServiceItem.partNumber` exists explicitly to "enable reorder and lifetime tracking", and `.parts` is a category on both `ServiceCategory` and `ExpenseCategory`. What is missing is the SHELF: a part bought now and fitted later has nowhere to wait, so today it is either an expense with no destination or a service line typed twice. J6 already has the journey and its metric ("shelf-suggested parts accepted >=40%"), and its fallback is already correct - "the shelf is an optimization, never a gate". **What competitors do here, measured 2026-09-06**: aCar records part specifications and MyFuelLog2 records purchased parts with manufacturer and place bought, but **nobody in the category ships a real inventory** - no stock counts, no fitted-state, no lifetime. It is genuinely unowned, which is the same reason to be careful: it may be unowned because owners do not want it. **The fix:** **A part is bought, then fitted** - two events on one row, not two records. Buying is the expense that already exists; fitting attaches it to a `ServiceRecord` through `usedParts`, and the shelf is simply the parts bought and not yet fitted. **Do not build a warehouse**: no quantities, no reorder points, no suppliers - the moment it needs a stock count it has become a different product (`docs/VISION.md` scope). **Prove the demand before the depth**: the cheapest version is the service form suggesting parts already on the shelf, which is exactly what J6's metric measures, and if acceptance is under that bar the feature is answered rather than expanded. Lifetime ("this filter lasted 18 400 km") derives from the odometer span between fitting and replacement, the same way `TireSet` mileage already does - reuse that, do not invent a second derivation | L1: a `.parts` expense with no service is on the shelf; attaching it to a service removes it and leaves the cost on the expense, never duplicated into the service total (hard rule 4's sibling - one cost, one place). L1: fitted-to-replaced odometer span yields the part's lifetime, and an unknown span says "unknown" rather than estimating (the `TireSet` rule). L4 `ServiceEntryUITests`: the form suggests a shelf part and one tap fits it. **Vacuous traps**: asserting the shelf list renders rather than what fitting CHANGES; double-counting the cost and calling it linked; a fixture where the part was bought and fitted the same day, which hides the whole point |
| **[ ]** PJ.53 | **[v1.x]** **There is no widget, no Shortcut and no lock-screen door - the habit surface the whole capture design assumes.** `VISION.md` lists "Widgets, Shortcuts, Siri" at v1.x and J3's opening beat is literally "lock-screen widget / app opens on capture", yet `project.yml` has **no widget extension target** (verified 2026-09-06) and the app donates no App Intent. J3's metric is "median capture-to-save under 15 s", which is unreachable while reaching the camera means finding an app icon. Competitors ship this: widgets in aCar, Fuelio and My Cars; Siri Shortcuts in Road Trip MPG (`docs/COMPETITORS.md`). The core was built as a reusable package for exactly this (P0.1: "widgets/extensions can reuse it"). **The fix:** **An App Intent first, a widget second.** `LogFillUp` (and `TypeItManually`) as App Intents makes the action available to Siri, Spotlight, the Shortcuts app and the Action button at once, and a widget is then a launcher for the same intent - one action, several doors. **Both doors, as always** (hard rule 15): the widget carries capture AND type-it, not capture alone. **Decide what an intent may WRITE**: the honest answer is nothing - it opens the app on the right screen - because a fill-up saved without the user seeing the fields is hard rule 13 inverted. Say so in the row when you build it | L4: the widget's two controls open the capture surface and the manual form respectively. L1: the App Intent is donated and resolves without the app running. **Vacuous traps**: asserting the widget target builds; a widget that only launches the app with no destination; shipping capture-only, which makes the widget a rule-15 seam of its own. Screenshots of the widget in both sizes, EN+RU |
| **[ ]** PJ.54 | (J3 the habit surface after a fill) **[v1.x]** **Nothing offers to log the fill-up you just made.** The moment a fill-up is knowable is when the driver gets back in and drives off, and the app is silent then; the user has to remember, later, at a screen. This is also the only credible attack on the **odometer friction** `VISION.md:191` names - the one field no receipt carries - because the prompt can arrive while the car is still around the user. Depends on PJ.53's App Intent: without it there is nothing for an automation to run. **The honest scope, and its limit:** **the trigger is a CarPlay or car-Bluetooth DISCONNECT, and the user wires it.** iOS personal automations are the user's to create - an app cannot install one silently - so what we ship is the intent, an "Add to Shortcuts" affordance, and copy that explains the recipe in one line. **"Near a fuel station" is NOT in this row and should not be smuggled into it**: there is no Shortcuts trigger for "any fuel station", so it would need CoreLocation region monitoring - the app imports no CoreLocation today, and adding it changes the privacy nutrition label we just submitted (`docs/STORE.md` §6 declares no location collected). That is a product decision with a store consequence, not an implementation detail; if it is ever wanted it gets its own row and its own argument. **The fix:** ship the intent and the recipe; measure whether people wire it before considering location | L4: the intent runs from the Shortcuts app and lands on the capture surface with the car preselected. L1: the intent is available with the app backgrounded. Manual check on the floor device: create the disconnect automation, drive, and confirm the prompt arrives once and not on every stop. **Vacuous traps**: asserting the shortcut appears in the app's own UI rather than in Shortcuts; claiming a location trigger that CoreLocation absence makes impossible; a prompt that fires on every Bluetooth disconnect, which trains the user to dismiss it |

## RV · Reviewer bugs (first TestFlight build, 2026-09-02/03)

*Found by running build **1.0.0+466** on a real device against the live backend, and by reading
production logs. Every row here is something no test caught, because each sits in a seam the suites
do not span: a client/server contract, a UIKit lifetime, a tier default, or a screen nobody drove on
hardware. `RV.1`-`RV.5`, `RV.7`-`RV.9` and `RV.11`-`RV.16` are fixed; `RV.10`, `RV.17`-`RV.20`, `RV.22`-`RV.26` and `RV.28`-`RV.35` are open.*

| # | Bug | Check |
|---|---|---|
| **[ ]** RV.148 **[v1.1]** | **Stays deferred (product owner, 2026-09-11, option 3).** (J8 the monthly glance, as a push) **The monthly-summary push still sums a rate-pending month as if it were complete.** Found by the [RV.147] agent and correctly left outside its fence. `MonthlySummaryNotification.swift:178-181` sums the known home amounts and pushes the figure even when the month still holds rate-pending rows; an ALL-pending month correctly produces no push, so the gap is exactly the partial case. This is the **fifth** instance of the shape [RV.106], [RV.112], [RV.145] and [RV.147] have each fixed on their own surface - and the first one that leaves the app and lands on a lock screen, where the user cannot see the footnote that would qualify it | **Decide the honest treatment for a PUSH, which is not the same question as for a tile.** A notification has no room for a footnote and no way to update itself once read, so the two candidates are: suppress the push while the month is partial (consistent with the all-pending case, which already suppresses), or send it with the figure marked as partial in the body copy. Reuse `LogStream.MonthTotal.Accumulator` - the same seam every other surface now goes through - and record the decision in `docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md` with its reason | L1: a partially-pending month does not push a bare figure. L1: an all-pending month still produces no push (the regression guard). L1: a fully converted month is unchanged, to the cent. L1: the classification comes from the shared accumulator, not a parallel sum. **Vacuous traps**: asserting the push happened rather than what its body says; a fixture with no pending rows, which passes today; fixing the copy while the underlying figure is still an understated sum |
| **[ ]** RV.168 | no-scenario: how briefs ask for proof - process, not product **Two weaknesses in how briefs ask for proof, identified 2026-09-09 by an external review of this project's own practice.** (1) **The oracle is unsourced.** A checks row says what the expected value IS but never where it CAME FROM, so a plausible wrong number can be written into a test and frozen - which happened: `MoneyBackfillServiceTests` asserted `costPerKm == 0.1` for a window whose pending row was skipped, canonising the defect [RV.147] later removed. Writing the assertion first does not make it right; it freezes a mistake earlier. (2) **The agent chooses its own mutation.** Briefs ask an agent to prove a test's teeth by breaking a line and watching it go red. Choosing the mutation proves sensitivity to *that edit*, not that the assertion encodes the requirement - and the easy mutation is the one that gets chosen | **(1) Every numeric or behavioural expectation in a checks row names its ORACLE**: a domain rule (`docs/SCHEMA.md` §x), an independent calculation shown in the row, or a hand-verified real fixture. *"Expected 150.00 because the receipt's KOKKU line says so"* is an oracle; *"expected 33.96"* is not. **(2) The BRIEF names the mutation**, targeting the row's headline claim, and the agent reports the result - it does not pick. `docs/DEFECT-PATTERNS.md` records both weaknesses; `agents/briefs/TEMPLATE.md` is where the change lands. **Do not adopt automated mutation testing** - it was considered and rejected for cost: a 60-second Swift suite times hundreds of mutants is hours per run, and the targeted version gets most of the value. Record that decision so it is not re-proposed | L1: `TEMPLATE.md` carries both rules and the briefs README points at them. Manual: the next three briefs name their mutation and source their oracles - **check them, that is the acceptance**. **Vacuous traps**: adding the words to the template and not applying them to the next brief; naming a mutation so trivial it proves nothing; treating 'the row says so' as an oracle - the row is where the wrong number would live |
| **[ ]** RV.169 | no-scenario: a source-scan guard over the re-homing seam **A second re-homing path could be written and nothing would fail.** Named by the [RV.167] agent when asked which source-scan guards are worth a row, and it is the shape [RV.144] and [RV.143] both live in: a `Money` pair's snapshot fields (`homeCurrency`, `homeAmount`, `rateDate`) are supposed to be written in one place, because hard rule 3 makes a snapshot immutable and a backfill fill-blanks-only. Nothing enforces the seam - a second writer would re-home silently and the rule would be broken by assignment, not by argument | Extend the [RV.167] idiom: flag direct assignment to a `Money` pair's snapshot fields outside the owning files. **Confirm the seam first** - `Money.swift` and `RateStore` are the hypothesis, not the finding; the guard is only worth writing if the tree actually has one owner today. Grep: `rg -n "\.homeCurrency\s*=\|\.homeAmount\s*=\|\.rateDate\s*=" ios/Sources ios/App/Sources` | L1: a hand-written fixture assigning `homeAmount` outside the seam is flagged; the real owner is not. L1: today's tree passes, or every violation is a reasoned exception. **Vacuous traps**: the [RV.167] trap in a new suit - a pattern tuned to whatever the tree has today; an allowlist without reasons; scanning core and missing `ios/App/Sources` |
| **[~]** RV.181 | **UN-SKIPPED 2026-09-11 (product owner, from the latest release build): *"still can't share photo or export data"* - and the evidence path, the diagnostics export, goes through the same share sheet. Briefed: present from the window's top-most controller instead of a nested SwiftUI sheet host, `ShareLink` for single-item photo/PDF shares, and the `share` outcome readable in the on-screen diagnostics preview so the device evidence can be a screenshot. (J13 export - and J8b sharing a receipt) **Nothing is ever dispatched to a share destination - EVERY share in the app.** Product owner, 2026-09-10: *"I can see the share proposal, select a destination, but in the end, nothing is dispatched to the destination."* **Cause, pinned to a pattern rather than a line**: `UIActivityViewController` is hosted as the ROOT of a SwiftUI `.sheet` through `ActivityView: UIViewControllerRepresentable` (`Shared/ActivityView.swift:16-22`) at **every** call site - `DiagnosticsPreviewView.swift:38` (the diagnostics bundle), `AttachmentViewerView.swift:135` (a receipt photo or PDF), `ExportFlow.swift:42` (the account and per-car exports, PJ.36/PJ.38), `ImportWizardView.swift:542` (PJ.20 "send us the file"). An activity controller must be **presented BY** a view controller; hosted as a sheet root it is presented by SwiftUI's hosting controller, so when the user picks a destination the chosen activity has no presenter for its own UI (Mail compose, Messages, the Files browser) and the hand-off dies silently - the sheet simply closes. `ExportFlow` compounds it with `.presentationDetents([.medium, .large])` on the activity controller itself. **Why nothing caught it**: `PJ.36`/`PJ.38` ship screenshots of the share sheet OPEN, and the L4s assert it appears - the sheet appearing is the half that works. **This is a hard rule 8 failure in the export direction and it contradicts a launch commitment**: *export always free* is a product promise (`VISION.md`), `DELETE /account` points at export as the way to keep your data, and today no share leaves the app | **Present the controller instead of hosting it.** The representable should own a plain host `UIViewController` and present the `UIActivityViewController` from it (in `updateUIViewController`, guarded so it presents once), or the call site should use SwiftUI `ShareLink` where the payload is a single item. **Fix it in the ONE shared seam** - `ActivityView` is already the single door, which is why this is one row and not five - and **drop the `presentationDetents`**. Check `completionWithItemsHandler` still fires exactly once so `RV.17`'s shape-only logging survives. **Verify on a DEVICE or a simulator with a real destination** (Files/Save to Files is the cheapest): the acceptance is that a file ARRIVES, not that a sheet opened | L4: sharing reaches a destination and the artefact exists afterwards - assert the RESULT, never the sheet. **A test that asserts the sheet appeared passes on today's broken code**, which is exactly why this shipped. L1: `completionWithItemsHandler` fires once per share, `completed` true only when an activity ran. L4: all five surfaces - diagnostics, photo, PDF, account export, per-car export, send-file - go through the fixed seam. **Vacuous traps**: asserting the sheet is presented (today's state); testing with `UIActivityViewController` in a unit test, which cannot exercise the hand-off; fixing one call site and leaving the shared seam, or the seam and leaving `presentationDetents`; trusting the existing PJ.36/PJ.38 screenshots as evidence - they show the half that already worked | **NARROWED 2026-09-10 by the product owner: iOS 26 on a real iPhone 13** - and the simulator is iOS **26.5**, so this is NOT a version difference, it is **device vs simulator**. That explains why nothing reproduced: a simulator carries almost no share extensions, and *Save to Files* - the destination the agent's L4 used - is **in-process**, so it needs no presenter and works on the broken shape too. **The failing destinations are out-of-process app extensions** (Mail, Telegram, WhatsApp, AirDrop): the chosen extension must put its OWN UI up, and that is exactly what a host torn down by SwiftUI cannot provide. **So the fix addresses the right mechanism and the test was green for the most misleading possible reason.** **This row cannot be verified on a simulator at all** - the acceptance is a real device, and the L4 should be marked as covering only the in-process case. **THE CAUSE IS NOT ESTABLISHED, and the diagnosis in this row's Cause column is WITHDRAWN (2026-09-10, after review).** "An activity hosted as a sheet root has no presenter" is **not supported**: UIKit forwards a presentation up the parent hierarchy, so the hosting controller IS a presenter. The counter-evidence was in hand and misread - *Save to Files completes under BOTH the old and the new shape*, which was recorded above as "the test was green for the most misleading possible reason" when it is equally evidence that the old shape was not broken in the way claimed. **What shipped (2026-09-10) is HARDENING, not a fix**, and the row stays OPEN: (1) the seam now presents from a plain host and **latches only after UIKit ACCEPTS the presentation** - the first attempt set the latch before presenting, which would have turned a dropped presentation into a permanent blank sheet with no retry, a defect the change itself introduced; (2) the seam captures the **whole** completion tuple as `ShareOutcome` (activity type, completed, error domain and code - shape only, hard rule 12) and logs `failed` as an outcome distinct from `cancelled`, documented in `docs/LOGGING.md` -> Shares. **That second half is the actual deliverable**: the reason this could not be diagnosed is that a share which failed at its destination was logged identically to one the user closed. **The next device report is now diagnosable from a diagnostics bundle.** Verification still requires the product owner's physical iPhone 13 - `RV181ShareHandoffUITests` passes on both shapes and its header now says so rather than claiming it "fails on the broken seam". **SKIPPED by the product owner, 2026-09-10.** The row stays open and the symptom is unfixed; it is not being worked and is out of the dispatch queue. **Nothing further is agent work**: it does not reproduce on the simulator - the failing destinations are out-of-process extensions - so the only remaining evidence is one share attempt from a physical device. What `ae775cf` bought is that such a report is now *answerable*: a share that fails at its destination logs `failed` with its activity type and error domain/code, where before it was indistinguishable from a cancel. Pick this up when a device is available, or when a second user reports it. | **SHIPPED 2026-09-11, awaiting the device.** `SharePresenter` walks the key window's root through `presentedViewController` to the top and presents the `UIActivityViewController` THERE - no SwiftUI sheet host, nothing SwiftUI does can tear the destination down. Every door - diagnostics, export, per-car export, import's *send us the file*, the receipt photo - calls it from the button action; `ActivityView`/`ShareHostController` and every `.sheet`-hosted share are gone. `ShareOutcome` and the shape-only log are byte-identical. **Deliberate deviation, accepted**: the brief asked `ShareLink` for the photo; the agent declined because `ShareLink` exposes no completion, so a photo share could never log its outcome - which would break the device step this row depends on. One seam, four doors. The diagnostics preview already carries the breadcrumb ring, so the `share` outcome line is on screen without a share; pinned by an L4. **Mutation (orchestrator-run)**: presenting from the root instead of `topMost(from:)` turns `RV181ActivityViewTests` red - *"the root must not present over its own sheet"*, presented count 2 not 1 - restored byte-identical, 10/10 with the seam-source guard. **The simulator cannot prove a destination dispatch**; the agent said so and so does this cell. The full package run at commit time carried 38 failures in the corpus and `RV.157` suites and a lint error in `ZZTempCorpusDumpTests.swift` - **all the concurrent corpus agent's uncommitted fixtures and temp file, none of them this row's**; the agent's own `gate.sh` run before those landed was 2026/245 green. **Closes on the owner's device step**: one photo share to Messages on the iPhone 13, then Settings -> About -> diagnostics preview, screenshot of the `attachmentViewer.share` line. Found: `RV181ShareHandoffUITests/testExportReachesSaveToFiles` races the Files copy - flaky, passes alone; no row. **Device report 2026-09-15 (product owner, iPhone 13, build 1344)**: the diagnostics text share reached **AirDrop** and did **not** reach **Telegram**. AirDrop is an out-of-process destination too, so the presenter is not the mechanism; the failure is destination-specific. The bundle pasted was generated BEFORE the share, so it carries no `diagnostics.share` outcome line - the evidence this row was hardened to produce. **Next step, owner**: after the Telegram attempt, open Settings → About → diagnostics preview again and screenshot the two `diagnostics.share` lines (one `failed`/`cancelled` with activity type and error domain/code, one `completed`). **Hypothesis to test against that line**: the diagnostics payload is one `String` of ~60 log lines plus counts (`DiagnosticsPreviewView.swift:84`, `items: [text]`), well over Telegram's 4096-character message limit, and its share extension refuses over-long text where AirDrop, Notes and Mail accept it; if the outcome line agrees, the fix is to share the bundle as a `diagnostics.txt` file URL (every destination takes a file) with the text kept as a second item, in the one seam. **File share shipped 2026-09-15** (flash): `DiagnosticsShare.swift` writes the preview to `<caches>/tankbook-diagnostics-<yyyyMMdd-HHmm>.txt` under `completeUntilFirstUserAuthentication`, sweeps stale bundles, hands the sheet `[fileURL]` alone (a `[fileURL, text]` pair lets an extension take the text and ignore the file), logs `diagnostics.share kind=file`, removes the file after the completion handler. No other door shares a bare `String`. `LOGGING.md`, `ERRORS.md`. **Checks in the orchestrator's hands**: `gate.sh` 0 at 2118 / 262, app bundle 273, lint 0; `RV181ShareHandoffUITests` 3/3 (agent); `RV181DiagnosticsFileShareTests` 4/4. **Mutation (orchestrator-run)**: `presenter([url])` → `presenter([text])` turns `testTheShareItemIsASingleProtectedTxtFileMatchingThePreview` red (*the share item must be a file URL, not the raw string*); restored byte-identical. Both frames opened: the sheet shows the file row *"tankbook-diagnostics-20260915-15… · Text Document · 5 KB"*, EN and RU. **Row stays `[~]`**: closes on the owner's iPhone - one diagnostics share to Telegram arrives, then the `diagnostics.share` line in the preview. Noticed in the RU frame, not this row: the preview's nav title *Предпросмотр* truncates to *Предпросм…* between the two bar buttons - filed as [RV.287]. **Second device report 2026-09-15 (after the file-share commit, on build 1344 which predates it)**: a RECEIPT PHOTO shared to Telegram did not arrive either, while Copy + paste into Telegram worked. So the failure is not text-length alone - the photo door hands the sheet a `UIImage`/file, not a string - and the file-share change may not be the whole fix. Telegram is the one destination that fails; AirDrop (out-of-process) and Copy (in-process) both work. **The evidence now exists in the app**: since `ae775cf` every share logs `<surface>.share outcome=failed reason=activity=<type> error=<domain>#<code>`; the owner's Telegram attempt on 1344 wrote an `attachmentViewer.share` line. **Owner, next step**: Settings → About → diagnostics preview, screenshot the `attachmentViewer.share` and `diagnostics.share` lines. That line decides between the remaining candidates: Telegram's extension rejecting the item type (`completed=false` with an error), the extension crashing on the item's size (a full-resolution receipt; a `NSExtension` error domain), or the hand-off never reaching it (`cancelled` with `activityType` set). |

| **[ ]** RV.124 **[v1.1]** | (J7b tires - the seasonal advisory) **Tell a driver when the season turns: tyre-change advisory from forecast and local law.** Product owner, 2026-09-07: a background job checks the **10-14 day forecast**, and when the mean temperature is about to cross the **+8 C** rule (the standard change-over recommendation, both directions) the user is told to swap summer/winter tyres - *"we know the country of a driver now, so we can deliver a precise notification. Sometimes, even a city."* Tankbook already has the two halves this needs: **tire sets** with swap history (P3.3) and a **reminder + notification** pipeline. **The country is now derivable** without asking - [RV.115]'s chain is receipt in hand, then the user's own history, then device region, then `detectedCountry` | **The obvious shape is FORBIDDEN, and the doc says so - read this before designing.** `docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md:11` states it as a hard rule: **"No user-visible remote push exists at all in v1, and no marketing push will ever exist"** - the only remote push is the content-free silent sync nudge. A server that decides "this user needs winter tyres" and pushes it would also have to know where the user is and what tyres they own, which is hard rule 9 (the server never reads domain meaning) broken on top. **So the architecture inverts, and it is the one this repo already uses twice**: a **backend job** polls the forecast provider (the API key stays server-side, `docs/SECURITY.md`) and publishes a small **seasonal-advisory pack** as public, ETag'd **reference data** - the same contract as `GET /reference/fuel-price-bands` and [RV.115]'s station brands, carrying per-region rows and taking no query parameters. The **device** matches its region, decides whether it applies, and schedules a **local** notification through the existing reminder path. The server never learns who owns winter tyres. **Two inputs, not one**: the weather crossing AND the **statutory window** (Russia and Estonia mandate winter tyres by date; Germany's rule is situational) - a legal deadline is not a forecast and must not be presented as one. **A schema gap blocks the useful half**: `TireSet` is `{ id, vehicleId, name, purchaseExpenseId }` (`Entities.swift:406-414`) - **no season and no 'currently fitted'** - so today the app cannot say "swap from summer to winter", only "it is the season". Decide whether to add them or to keep the advisory generic, and say which. **Quiet, twice a year, opt-out** - [RV.121] is the standing lesson about a card that shouts; a seasonal nudge that fires more than twice a year is a bug. **Degrade to silence**: no pack, no network, no location - nothing happens and nothing errors (hard rule 1) | L1: the crossing rule fires once per direction per season for a fixture forecast, and NOT on a single warm day inside winter. L1: a statutory window and a weather crossing produce different messages, and the statutory one names the date. L1: no region match yields silence, not a default region. L2: the pack is public, ETag'd and carries no user identifier. L4: the notification is local, dismissible, and switchable off in Settings. **Vacuous traps**: sending it as a remote push, which the hard rule above forbids; letting the server decide per user, which needs their location and their tyres; asserting the pack downloads rather than that the device decided correctly from it; firing on every cold day instead of on the seasonal crossing; testing only the summer-to-winter direction |
| **[ ]** RV.123 **[v2]** | **Transfer a car to another user - the acquisition loop.** Product owner, 2026-09-07: *"interesting and viral feature for 2.x - transfer a car from one to another user. This can help one day to acquire more users."* Selling a car today ends at archive or delete (J13, [RV.98]); the buyer starts from nothing and the history dies with the seller's account. A transfer hands over the car **and its log** - which is exactly the moment the new owner has a reason to install the app, so the feature is a growth loop, not only a convenience | **Design it as a MOVE with an explicit handshake, and decide the five things that make it safe.** (1) **What moves**: the vehicle, its entries, reminders, tire sets and attachments - and what does NOT (the seller's other cars, obviously; their account, their stations?). (2) **What the seller keeps**: a sold car's history is also the seller's own spending record, so decide between a true move, a copy, or a move that leaves the seller a read-only archive - and say why (hard rule 8 says nothing is lost silently, and losing five years of one's own spend on tapping 'transfer' would be exactly that). (3) **The recipient may have NO account** - that is the acquisition moment and the flow must survive it: an invite that outlives the install, and a transfer that cannot be claimed twice. (4) **The server side is a new capability**: moving records between accounts rewrites `account_id` on rows the server still never READS, so hard rule 9 holds in spirit - but it is an account-state change with its own failure modes, and **blobs are per-account with per-account dedupe** ([RV.44]/P4.3), so the attachments must move or be re-keyed rather than dangle. It needs a written decision in `docs/SYNC.md` and `docs/API.md` the way the four storage decisions did. (5) **It must be reversible or confirmed like a deletion** - a mistaken transfer is worse than a mistaken delete, because the data is now in someone else's account and the 30-day undo window cannot reach it | L2: a transfer moves every vehicle-scoped row exactly once, the recipient sees the whole history, and the seller's OTHER cars are untouched. L2: a claim link cannot be used twice, and an unclaimed transfer expires. L2: attachments resolve for the recipient and are not orphaned for the seller. L4: the seller's confirmation states exactly what leaves and what stays. **Vacuous traps**: testing with a car that has no attachments, which hides the blob half; asserting the vehicle moved without asserting the entries and reminders did; a transfer that silently destroys the seller's own spending history; testing only a recipient who already has an account, which skips the acquisition case the feature exists for |

## OB · Observability **[v1.0.x]** - the device can say what it did

**Defined 2026-09-05 (product owner).** Not a new backlog: this groups four rows that already
existed as `PR.9`, `PR.10`, `PR.11` and `PR.12` and names why they belong together. **The evidence
for the grouping is this week's bug hunt.** RV.58, RV.59, RV.60, RV.65 and RV.66 were all diagnosed
from **server** logs, because the device emits almost nothing - and two of them needed the
orchestrator to read client source to guess what the app had done. One of those guesses (RV.58's
cross-account claim) was **wrong and had to be retracted**, precisely because no client-side record
existed to check it against.

The cluster's value is not a feature. It is that the next production report should be answerable
from the device's own account of itself, instead of inferred from what the server happened to see.
**SH.3, the human floor walk, will generate exactly that kind of question**, which is the argument
for landing at least `OB.2` before it.

Build order, and it is a real dependency chain, not a preference:

| Order | Row | Was | Why here |
|---|---|---|---|
| **OB.1** | Error codes on the wire | `PR.9` | A stable `code` is what every later layer maps from; a status alone cannot name a next step (hard rule 7). RV.65's new sign-in message is the first consumer. |
| **OB.2** | Emit the defined events and async edges | `PR.10` | The foundation - nothing can be exported or persisted that was never emitted. Highest value alone: `NetRequest/Response` would have shown RV.59's duplicate config fetch and RV.65's doubled 53 KB upload from the client side. |
| **OB.3** | Persist last sync date and last failure | `PR.12` | Needs OB.1's `code` to record a failure meaningfully. The natural sibling of RV.58's revoked-device card - the state survives a relaunch instead of vanishing. |
| **OB.4** | Diagnostics export end to end | `PR.11` | Needs OB.2's events to have anything worth exporting; it is the user-facing delivery of the other three. |

The four rows keep their `PR.*` ids and their own text - **this section is the ordering and the
rationale, not a duplicate backlog.** Marking any of them done marks the OB step done.

## PU · Pump reader – a trained seven-segment reader, not OCR (2026-09-18)

*The product owner's decision of 2026-09-18: pump-display capture gets its own recognition library
instead of relying on Vision OCR alone. `docs/EXTRACTION.md` → "The pump reader" carries the design
and the reasoning; this table carries the checks. The reader is a **segment-level** classifier
(7 segments + decimal point per glyph, decoded by lookup) fed by a panel locator and a fixed-pitch
digit slicer, trained on **synthetic** renders only - every one of the 114 real pump fixtures stays
held-out, which is what keeps `PumpPhotoGate` an honest measurement. Training code is Python under
`ml/pump-reader/`, outside every gate except its own `pytest`; the exported `.mlpackage` and the
Swift side ride the ordinary iOS gate. Hard rules 13 and 15 are unchanged: the reader produces
suggestions, `nil` beats a confident-wrong value, and typing stays a peer path. Branch
`pump-reader`, worktree `../fuel-counter-ios-pump-reader` (product owner asked for one).*

| ID | Task | Checks (done when...) |
|---|---|---|
| **[x]** PU.1 | **SHIPPED 2026-09-19** - `ml/pump-reader/` on Python 3.12, 9 tests (the orchestrator added `test_profiles_differ_geometry.py`: the agent's colour-driven floor stayed green under the named geometry mutation, so a lit-segment-mask test now carries it - red at 0.0000 under the clone, green restored). Finding for PU.3: display technology is bound to make (wayne/dresser = LED, scheidt = VFD) by guess; sample it independently with an LCD-heavy prior, since technology is a palette and not a geometry. (J4 pump display photo) **The synthetic renderer.** `ml/pump-reader/render/`: draws seven-segment glyphs from a **make profile** (segment width, slant, inter-segment gap, glyph pitch, on/off colour, LCD vs LED vs VFD look) for at least the five makes the corpus holds - Gilbarco, Wayne, Dresser, Scheidt, Tokheim - and augments with glare, blur, perspective, LCD ghosting, canopy reflection, sensor noise and exposure. Emits a glyph crop plus its **8-bit segment label** (a–g + dp) and, at window level, a rendered number row plus glyph boxes. Deterministic under a seed. `python -m pump_reader.render --count N --seed S --out DIR` | `pytest ml/pump-reader` exit 0 with tests that assert: a fixed seed reproduces byte-identical output; every one of the 12 glyph classes (0–9, blank, dp-only) appears in a 1 000-sample draw; a label decodes back to the digit it was rendered from; each make profile produces a visibly different glyph (pixel-diff above a floor, oracle: the profile constants). Mutation named in the brief: zero the slant of one profile, the profile-difference test must go red |
| **[x]** PU.2 | **SHIPPED 2026-09-19** - 114 fixtures, 456 windows (342 transaction fields + 114 board cells), authored by the orchestrator from grid overlays at about +-1 % of the image edge; `scripts/pump-windows-check.py --check` exits 0 and goes red on a mutated string. Three declared exceptions, each a corpus fact worth knowing: `pump-031` the DISPLAY shows 32,58 while `expected.csv` asserts the receipt's discounted 32.50 (a pump reader is right to read 32.58 - the CSV scores the wrong artefact for that cell); `pump-072` the transaction price 1.894 is not on the board at all (loyalty price); `pump-067` the total is only partly legible through glare. (J4 pump display photo) **Number-window annotations for the real corpus - the held-out glyph set and the locator's oracle.** `Spike/ReceiptSpike/fixtures/pump/windows.json`: for every fixture, the quadrilateral of each number window (total / volume / price, in display order) and its ground-truth string with decimal point. | **[x]** PU.53 | (J4 pump display photo; F2 scan recognised WRONG data) **The live path never learns the photo's orientation, and is scored at the wrong one.** Found by the product owner in the compare view, 2026-09-22: `pump-019` carries `rotationCW: 90` in `windows.json` - the display is sideways in the frame - and the live path is run at 0 because nothing tells it otherwise. The agent also caught a defect in the brief's own search rule - scored as specified it picks **270** on `pump-019` and `pump-021`, both wrong; gating on row confidence first flips both to 90. | **[~]** PU.54 | (J4 pump display photo; F2 scan recognised WRONG data) **The price becomes optional (decision 11), and the board stops standing in for it silently.** **Product owner, 2026-09-22**: *"the price per unit is not always available... found total, found volume. (b) **the board is not always the price**: `pump-300` pays 27.87 for 13.70 L (2.034 a litre) while the board reads 2.019 / 2.069 / 2.079 / 2.219 - a loyalty discount - and the board-as-price tier closes on a price the customer did not pay, producing a CONFIDENT WRONG total. `pump-266` is the same shape (board 1.919, paid 1.839). Both are declared artefacts today; in the app they would be F2. The tier needs a rule for when a board cell may stand in for the price - or must abstain | L5: the live committed count RISES (the 26 `boardFoundNoPrice` stills are the population) and precision holds at 0.99 - both reported; the implied-price guard's own false-accept rate is measured on the heldout stills whose CSV asserts a price. | **[ ]** PU.55 | (J4 pump display photo; F2 scan recognised WRONG data) **The clip guard: a strip whose edge carries cut glyph ink abstains.** PU.52's first-ranked fix (`agents/reviews/PU.52-REVIEW-WHY-IT-LOWERED-qwen.md` §4). `pump-092` under the PU.48 detector commits `3.0` / `191.55` for 30.00 / 1915.5 - not one clipped digit but three small failures the law's decimal freedom assembles: the box cut the litres' trailing cell (`30.00` -> `30.0`; the d48 litres box is 0.4629..0.6797 against the oracle's 0.5035..0.6939), the total's decimal mark was missed, and its last digit read 6 for 5; the law then placed the points so `3.0 x 63.85 = 191.55` closed. The arithmetic is scale-blind (PU.32's review named this), the cell count is self-consistent (3 cells is a legal litres row) and an object detector cannot see a sub-pixel clip - **the one signal that survives all three is ink running off the strip's edge**. `PumpReader.sliceDetectedOrOriginal` already has the recovery half (widen by `detectedMarginHorizontal`, keep the wider slice only when it finds at least as many cells) and, when the widening does NOT recover, silently falls back to the clipped slice - that fallback is the missing abstention. **What to build**: measure edge ink on the warped strip (the ink band's first/last column against the strip's edge, in the slicer's own polarity) and, when the widened slice did not recover a cell AND the plain slice's edge carries cut ink, refuse the row with a named reason rather than reading it | L5: under the PU.48 candidate the live `WRONG` lines are EMPTY and precision is 1.000 (today 36 / 0.944 with `pump-092` and `pump-096` wrong); the committed count falls by at most the wrong cells and every still that was correct stays correct - print `cellsPerRow` per still before and after to prove no correct row flipped. Under the shipped detector the floor (43 / 1.000) is unchanged. L1: a synthetic strip whose glyph is cut at the edge is refused; one whose glyph merely touches the edge without being cut is kept (**the risk PU.13 measured: the slicer right-aligns, so 81.5 % of cells carry right-edge ink legitimately** - the guard must separate touching from cut, and the test is the place that proves it). **Named mutation**: make the guard fire on any edge ink - the touching-not-cut test goes red. **Vacuous traps**: a guard that fires on the legal right-edge ink and buys precision by refusing everything; measuring the guard only on `pump-092`; a threshold fitted per head | **INVESTIGATED 2026-09-22, NOTHING SHIPPED - the review's diagnosis does not reproduce** (`ml/pump-reader/REPORT.md` -> PU.55). The failure reproduces exactly (`pump-092` under the PU.48 candidate commits `3` / `191.55`, litres box `0.4709..0.6717` against the oracle's `0.5035..0.6939`), but the cell loss is a function of the box's **vertical** extent alone, not the horizontal clip: swapping only the box's height for the oracle's recovers the fourth cell (`30.01`), swapping only its left/right does not (`30.0`). And the signal the guard was to be built on is absent - the clipped strip's edge is *quieter* than the framed one (band-average edge ink 0.028/0.018 against the oracle's 0.009), because the cut leaves the trailing zero's bar 5-14 px inside the edge. Both literal guards fail: firing on edge ink drops committed 41 -> 3, requiring a bar plus a gutter fires on nothing. **Re-scope**: the defect is the detector box's vertical extent - the detector half is PU.57's tight-IoU gate (shipped), the read half is a band trim before the pitch is computed, which no row owns. Also recorded: the brief's baseline was stale - the PU.48 candidate now reads **41 / 39 / 0.951** with `pump-092` the only wrong still, `pump-096` no longer commits wrong.

| **[ ]** PU.59 | (J4 pump display photo; F2 scan recognised WRONG data) **A pair no shown price validates commits with a caution, and the ship gate splits in two.** Decision 11's second amendment (product owner, 2026-09-22, `docs/EXTRACTION.md`), relaxing what PU.54 shipped. median IoU back to ~0.80 would put the fix in the data (verify more frames, refit tracker boxes), not the model. **ROUND 3 (2026-09-23): +-3/6 deg, HAND BOXES ONLY (`--hand-only`, `pu66c`: 249 stills + 60 verified frames, no tracker-boxed frames) - REFUSED, and it settles the label question.** Every detector on the machine, same 68 heldout stills (median IoU / recall@0.7 / false rows per photo / recall@0.5 / live off / live on refusal / presence kept-dropped-missed / 20 stills past 12 deg on refusal): shipped 0.797 / 0.734 / 0.632 / 0.869 / 47-47 / 59-59 / 157-27-6 / 3-3; DigitRows16 identical to the shipped model on every number; PU.48 first 0.768 / 0.667 / 0.853 / 0.865 / 40-38 / 52-49 / 159-25-6 / 0-0; PU.48 final 0.772 / 0.706 / 0.824 / 0.877 / 36-36 / 47-46 / 160-26-4 / 3-3; round 1 0.778 / 0.758 / 0.750 / 0.909 / 26-23 / 31-28 / 157-24-9 / 5-4; round 2 0.779 / 0.730 / 0.559 / 0.909 / 25-24 / 34-33 / 157-28-5 / 5-5; round 3 0.790 / 0.734 / 0.574 / 0.921 / 35-32 / 52-49 / 164-22-4 / 3-3. **Findings**: (1) removing the tracker's boxes is the largest single improvement in the table - round 2 to round 3 changes nothing else and lifts median IoU 0.779 -> 0.790, rows kept 157 -> 164 (best of all), live 25 -> 35 off and 34 -> 52 on refusal: frames boxed by the tracker rather than the owner measurably degrade the detector; (2) round 3 finds rows best and still frames slightly looser than the shipped model, and every retrain makes wrong readings on heldout where the shipped one makes none - the read stage is unforgiving of framing; (3) the recipe is hand boxes and the limit is their count (249 stills, 60 verified frames) - more owner-verified frames are the lever. **The shipped detector stays.** Candidates pu66, pu66b, pu66c remain in the annotator's picker.

Bundle size and Release latency against the 31 MB detector. |
| **[ ]** PU.77 | (J4 pump display photo) **A row-level sequence reader - spike, offline first.** The slicer's cell count is the verifier's largest drop (-17) and a class of six named failure shapes. Papers: Shi, Bai, Yao, *An End-to-End Trainable Neural Network for Image-based Sequence Recognition*, TPAMI 2017, arXiv:1507.05717 (CRNN); Graves et al., *Connectionist Temporal Classification*, ICML 2006; Jaderberg et al., *Spatial Transformer Networks*, NeurIPS 2015, arXiv:1506.02025 (learned rectification); Baek et al., *What Is Wrong With Scene Text Recognition Model Comparisons?*, ICCV 2019, arXiv:1904.01906 (CTC vs attention, rectification). Cycle (product owner, 2026-09-23, the PU.67 tranche): `agents/briefs/RESEARCH-TO-CODE.md` note in `agents/research/<row>.md` first where a paper is cited; build and gate by the orchestrator; `agents/briefs/REVIEW-PU-COMPLETENESS.md` by a reviewing agent; commit only on its COMPLETE verdict. | Offline only until it passes: trained on the train split's window strings + synthetic strips; string accuracy on the annotated tier's strips must beat the current 0.732 digit-only, and the law over its posteriors must commit >= 111 at >= 0.99 on the annotated tier - else stop before any Swift. A Swift integration is its own row. |
| **[ ]** PU.6 | (J4 pump display photo) **The ship decision, whichever way it goes.** If PU.5 clears the gate: the flag can turn on, the capture caption (PJ.12b) earns its pump sentence, `docs/VISION.md`'s feature table and `docs/EXTRACTION.md`'s pump paragraphs are rewritten from the new numbers. If it does not: the measured gap, by failure mode and by make, is written into EXTRACTION.md and the next row is filed from it - never "try harder" | Docs reconciled in the same change; `scripts/tasks-index.py --check` and `scripts/scenario-index.py --check` exit 0; J4's review (`REVIEW-SCENARIO`) is walked by the orchestrator before any status line is written |

| **[ ]** AG.14 | (J17 grounding the answer in what a general model cannot have) **Ground the answer in what a general model cannot have, and say so on the screen.** From the 2026-09-06 market read (`diagnostics/RESEARCH-agent-market.md`): no established fuel log ships conversational AI over the owner's records - Drivvo, Fuelio, Fuelly, aCar, Simply Auto, CarScope and Spritmonitor all lack it - but users already troubleshoot with free ChatGPT and Gemini and **say so in reviews**, so an undifferentiated car chatbot reads as repackaged search. The seven things buyers named as worth paying for are: their own fuel/expense/service history, VIN and manual grounding, live OBD data, guided tests with confidence, exact parts and diagrams, writing back to the log, and a safety escalation. **Tankbook owns two of them outright (history, write-back) and deliberately owns none of the diagnostic ones.** The tool catalogue (`AGENT.md` §4) is already the first; the draft tools are already the sixth | Every answer that used the user's data **shows which rows it read** - the card is the evidence, not decoration (`AGENT.md` numbers-from-tools rule). L4: an answer about spend opens the entries behind it. **Vacuous trap**: a citation line that names a tool without opening what it returned |
| **[ ]** AG.15 | no-scenario: pricing and metering policy, not a user journey **Price and metering, informed by what the market actually charges.** Measured 2026-09-06: the apps doing exactly this - Refuells **$1.99/mo or $19.99/yr**, Brimly **$2.99/mo** with 10 free questions a day, GarageHub **$7.99/mo** with ~15 free messages, MECH AI **$7.99/mo** with 3-5 a day, MyAutoLog **$3.99/mo** - all sit at **$2-8/month with a metered free tier**, while the $100/year products (FIXD, Carly) bundle hardware or human mechanics. **The review evidence is unambiguous on two failure modes** and both are ours to avoid: FIXD's worst reviews are about auto-renewal and cancellation rather than answer quality, and a paying MECH AI user found "unlimited" showing zero messages - **an entitlement bug erases willingness to pay faster than a bad answer does**. Feeds AG.12's paywall and `CONFIG.md`'s `llmQuota.agentTurns` | The quota the user sees equals the quota enforced, proven by a test that exhausts it and reads the number back. Cancellation is reachable from the paywall in one tap and says what happens to the log (nothing). **Vacuous traps**: asserting the paywall renders a price; testing the quota only at zero |
| **[ ]** AG.16 | (J14 the Ask tab) **The three answers the market says people actually ask, as the examples on an empty Ask tab.** Not invented: they are what the shipped competitors demonstrate (`diagnostics/RESEARCH-agent-market.md`) - Refuells answers "what did I spend last month", "how has efficiency changed", "which stations do I use"; GarageHub's Rev answers "what is overdue" and writes the log entry. Ours already have tools behind them (`spend`, `consumption`, `anomaly`, `reminders`, `stations`). **The empty state is the pitch**: a free user who never asks a question never learns what the tab is for, and `AGENT.md` §3 already says the examples are taps to the ordinary screens for a non-Pro user | L4 `AskUITests`: each example produces an answer whose card opens the screen the data lives on; for a non-Pro user each example opens that screen directly. **Vacuous trap**: asserting three buttons exist rather than what each produces |
| **[ ]** AG.17 | (F12 the agent is wrong - the safety stance) **A safety stance the reviews prove is needed, written before the first answer ships.** Recorded 2026-09-06 from the market read: an AI mechanic "inferred the wrong cause from a fault code" and a reviewer said it "could have caused expensive unnecessary repairs"; mechanics on r/MechanicAdvice reported generative AI doubling down on unsafe brake advice. `AGENT.md` §5's diagnosis framing and the answer gate exist for this, and this row is where they get their teeth: **braking, steering, fuel and high-voltage answers escalate to a professional and never end in a confident single cause**, uncertainty is stated rather than implied, and no answer recommends replacing a part from a code alone. **This is also the honest limit of our position**: we have no live diagnostic data, so diagnosis here is reasoning over history and symptoms, and the copy must not imply otherwise | L1: a symptom in a safety-critical category always produces the escalation line, whatever the model returned. L1: an answer with no supporting rows says so instead of asserting. **Vacuous traps**: testing the happy path only; asserting the disclaimer's presence rather than that it fires on the category |

## Working rules

- One task = one PR = code + its checks; the PR description quotes the task ID and its check list, ticked.
- A task discovering an undocumented behavior extends the owning doc in the same PR (CLAUDE.md rule).
- Snapshot baselines are reviewed like code; accuracy high-water marks only ratchet up.
